verifies the setting's syntax and precedence, not the hosted Intel driver itself.

and `package-smoke-opentt3d-prototype-package6-macos-arm64/`. Local prototype dependencies
require macOS26; hosted releases continue to target/audit macOS15. Fresh hosted
acceptance and public executable attachments remain pending.

## First hosted-package results and compatibility follow-up

Release`.4`run36292341479 does **not** publish downloads. The source job and native
build/test steps pass, followed by these retained packaging failures:

- Linux: Python3.11.2 rejects `TarFile.extractall(filter=...)`. Generated CPack/git
  archives now use filters when available and the original API otherwise. The
  regression extracts a real archive through both API shapes.
- Windows, all three architectures: the system-DLL regex assumed forward slashes.
  It now accepts either separator, with a CMake control proving system paths are
  excluded and app-local Vulkan DLL paths remain eligible.
- macOS: extracted Apple-silicon automatic Vulkan rendering/save passes on the
  Apple Paravirtual device. Explicit OpenGL there and automatic Intel startup time
  out. Existing `OPENTT3D_ALLOW_SOFTWARE_GL=1` now applies to Cocoa context creation
  as well as backend acceptance. Hidden fatal alerts go to stderr, and future
  timeout reports include the actual process sample. These changes still require
  hosted verification; the timeout cause is not yet established.

Evidence: `build-macos/playable-release-ci-{result.json,failures.log}` and
`playable-release4-ci/`. Linux's extracted-launch/fallback and collector checks,
Windows native package load/save and public attachments remain pending.

The follow-up passes all200 native tests,12 harness tests, both slash-direction
CMake controls, actionlint1.7.12 and a fresh extracted local macOS automatic-Vulkan/
explicit-OpenGL rendering-and-save pair. Evidence:`playable-fix-build.log`,
`playable-fix-harness.log`, `playable-windows-path-check.cmake`,
`playable-fix-package2.log` and `package-smoke-opentt3d-prototype-packaging-fix-*`.
Those local prototype archives contain the in-progress gold catalogue and host-only
macOS26 dependencies; they are not public release artifacts. Hosted source remains
the exact committed1,416-volume catalogue until gold is independently reviewed.

## Full-elapsed presentation smoothing and packaged first launches

A3,000ms presentation delay outside the game-state mutex reproduces the retained
electric-collector crash on the exact iron-ore Linux fixture: requested local
contact6.2301865 below mount6.625. Slow presentation allows the simulation to advance;
the old0.1-second cap prevented the analytic exponential filter from catching up.
Vehicle and Cab smoothing now receive the full elapsed presentation interval.
Collector exceptions retain engine/vehicle/part/iteration and original/rendered
world positions for further diagnosis.

- Native200/200 tests pass, including long-interval clearance and wrap-safe yaw.
- Matched120-frame controls with the same3,000ms injected presentation delay pass
  actual engine26 support and collector observations on OpenGL and Vulkan.
- The injection is completely removed; `src/video/video_driver.cpp` matches the
  preceding published source. All8 clean controls pass: both retained factory/ore
  crash saves on each backend, both development app bundles, and two9,000-frame
  engine26 live support/collector, selected-pose, tunnel and picking journeys.
- The03:27:10UTC reconciliation confirms10 successful background controls, stable
  clean source/executable/bundle hashes and all1,416 unchanged model hashes. Peak
  sampled memory is3,169,832,296bytes. Linux acceptance remains pending fresh CI.

Evidence:`build-macos/breadth-collector-present-three-second-before/`,
`breadth-collector-full-elapsed-delayed-*`, `breadth-collector-clean-*` and
`breadth-collector-repair-reconciliation.json`. The delayed runs prove correctness
under the reproduced stall; they are not performance measurements or final visual
approvals.

The playable-release workflow adds archive-content and pinned-graphics checks,
dependency relocation, macOS ad-hoc signatures, and extracted-package default-3D
and OpenGL rendering/save controls. Windows x64/x86 packages load and re-save their
title world with the extracted executable; ARM64 is cross-compiled. Local macOS
packaging passes both extracted-app controls: automatic Vulkan and explicit OpenGL
each render in3D and save without a renderer override or injected game resources.
The local DMG/ZIP are30,363,345/33,491,188bytes. This host's dependencies require
macOS26, accurately declared in its private test app; hosted public builds target
macOS15 and must pass the same per-library minimum-version audit. Public platform
results remain pending until the release workflow finishes.

First-launch review additionally found that macOS disabled hardware acceleration
by default and SDL Vulkan's priority11 exceeded the upstream probe's0…10 range.
Hardware rendering is now the macOS default and SDL Vulkan uses priority10. An
orderly unsupported-Vulkan result clears its probe marker and tries OpenGL; the
existing crash-on-a-prior-launch protection is retained. Linux package controls
explicitly hide every Vulkan ICD and require automatic OpenGL rendering/saving.
The new boundary entries cover only renderer startup settings and driver probing.

The initial package-smoke timeout was a harness mistake: upstream `-X` excludes
macOS's bundle Resources, unlike the normal user launch. The package test now uses
normal bundle lookup with isolated personal data, without injecting graphics or
enabling3D through the console. That failed run remains retained as
`build-macos/playable-first-launch-missing-bundle-resources/`.

The source-archive check verifies2,154 entries, game and authored-model source,
license/build instructions, and the exact pinned OpenGFX2 source payload against its
recorded digest. The workflow passes actionlint1.7.12. Local native200/200,
asset128/128 and harness11/11 checks pass; the137-file presentation boundary passes.
Evidence:`build-macos/playable-package4.log`, `package-smoke-{default,opengl}/`,
`playable-package-native4.log` and `playable-source-test/`.

processes. Crash/error logs are checked before sampling. Two new portable regressions
and the existing live-allocation guard pass; this diagnostic repair does not fix the

is claimed. Remaining iron-ore Linux jobs are active/queued at this audit.

  (Linux RSS+swap) every250ms and terminates on a sampled limit breach. It retains

- `memory-gl-before-600` aborts at6,204MiB after10.18seconds with RSS only2,684MiB;

  Allocation-stack evidence in `memory-gl-allocation-stacks` identifies source mesh
  capacity plus per-transform triangle/packed/indexed copies as large allocations.
  A first profiling attempt applied MallocStackLogging to Python itself and hit an
  OS uniquing-table assertion before starting the game; the retained successful
  profile enables it only for the game. Post-change lifetime verification is ongoing.

### Memory ownership fixes and bounded follow-up

- Packed visibility results are now shared immutable vectors between workers and
  renderer caches. Encoding releases the redundant original-index list. Catalogue
  meshes release unused vector growth capacity without changing any vertex bits.
- Canceled workers now wake and release old camera results even when no subsequent
  request arrives. Both backends retire wide-view visibility when zooming in/entering
  Cab. Native CTest passes186/186 in `memory-lifetime-ctest.log`, including actual
  weak-pointer release after repeated idle cancellation with one/two workers, plus
  cache-eviction ownership and unchanged packed geometry.
- GL instance records now use two retained, fence-paced texture buffers with
  subdata updates and capacity growth only when needed. The temporary full-GPU-wait
  diagnostic and upload-selection switch are removed. The full-wait experiment
  completed600 frames at15.115fps; it is not the retained implementation.
- CPU sharing alone (`memory-gl-shared-600`) still exceeded6GiB after30.16seconds.
  Stable GL uploads completed600 frames at a5,849.41MiB sampled peak; their initial
  6,000-frame attempt (`memory-gl-reuse-instances-6000`) still crossed6GiB after
  225.19seconds and is aborted evidence. Counters show nearly unchanged1.05GB live
  GPU pages but accumulating historical CPU transforms. GL now releases obsolete
  indexed transforms on each rebuilt stream, marks reused pages correctly, and
  forgets packed intermediates after indexing. It recognizes already-indexed data
  when deciding whether background preparation is needed.
- `memory-gl-lifetime-validation` hits its300-second timeout during the full vehicle
  matrix. Before that it passes16,968 voxel-tree views,5,712 distant views, five
  1025-instance edit cases,14,736 authored views,1176 body/916 ground cases and256
  joined views. Peak sampled footprint is5,009.86MiB. It is not a completed full run.
  Targeted instance verification now also exercises large mesh storage, resident
  composition and repeated1/513/2049/17/4097/1 instance-count changes on both slots,
  checking exact live palette/picking and bounded buffer capacity.
- Docker daemon queries timed out twice (120seconds and10seconds); Linux and
  no-Vulkan post-fix execution is currently unavailable. Earlier Linux checkpoints
  retain their original scopes. The first two memory probes predate corrected
  macOS CPU-timebase conversion; use their memory fields, not their CPU seconds.

## OpenGL visibility, palette lifetime and atlas precision (16:45 UTC September25)

- `OPENTT3D_VOXEL_CULL=1` now enables the conservative original-triangle path on GL
  as well as Vulkan. GL uses exact-product-qualified32-bit source vertices, float
  texture-buffer views for original metadata/normal bits, indexed visible references
  and the same original instance/rasterization/material path. Unsupported encodings,
  transforms, transparency and buffer limits retain the original mesh path. It works
  on GLSL1.50 without shader-bit-encoding or gpu-shader5 requirements.
- GL's queried native subpixel precision is4bits (Linux8); the larger conservative
  uncertainty retains132,748,500 of2,566,353,378 original references at the paused wide
  pose, with65,380,420 indexed vertices. No precision bound is lowered for performance.
  Exact-product and complete palette-template qualification remain mandatory.
- A per-layer stream cache prevents a child batch sharing its parent's mesh from
  retiring the parent's not-yet-submitted buffers. Geometry indexing is cached per
  camera/transform. Ordered pages of at most256 instances reuse complete matching
  subsequences after insertion/removal; local instance references rebase at draw time.
  Small interior fragments merge on later edits. Buffers are bounded by real GL
  limits and replaced objects retire under GL's submitted-command lifetime rules.
- `voxel-gl-stable-pages-matrix`, `voxel-vulkan-layer-edits-matrix` and Linux
  `voxel-gl32-stable-pages-live` pass16,968 lifecycle/palette/scale views,5,712 distant
  ordinary/large-origin/coincident parent-child views (99,220 visible pixels), and
  five edited1025-instance streams covering insertion, removed page anchors, reorder
  and live palette/ID changes. CPU indexing checks preserve corner/provoking order,
  instance separation and chunk resets. Native183/Linux184 C++ cases pass after the
  later UV test; assets83/harness7/boundary131/whitespace pass.
- `voxel-gl-stable-pages-world/full-mesh-comparison.json` matches all4,096,000 RGBA
  pixels of its current unculled GL control. Earlier nonindexed and indexed/paged
  comparisons also match; the initial indexed4.626fps candidate is a performance
  failure, not discarded evidence. Sustained current GL6,000-frame throughput is
  31.94751fps and remains below target; see `PERFORMANCE.md`.
- `build-linux-novulkan/voxel-gl-visibility-fallback` proves compilation with
  `OPTION_VULKAN=OFF`, GL3.2/GLSL150 with `GL_ARB_gpu_shader5` disabled, the correctness
  matrices and12 live frames plus warm-up. Its initial CTest181 unit cases pass while
  both upstream script regressions fail because this build lacks graphics. The
  retained `regression_*_output.txt` files record that failure. Installing the shared,
  checksum-verified Classic archive makes both reruns pass; no game or golden changes.
- The source/recent-palette caches now have an explicit eviction regression. A normal
  chart evicts the last palette's full page and can reuse its map-node address with
  the same palette ID. Clearing the direct pointer before page erasure prevents a
  stale chart being returned as a256-colour strip. Native
  `voxel-palette-eviction-validation` and the no-Vulkan checks pass this case.
- The one-pixel whole-world difference is reproduced with cache on/off and with
  culling on/off. `world-pixel-template-{plain,ground}` identifies Toyland house108,
  tile45,226,screen1016,470; model position/normal/UV/other hashes agree. Its absolute
  UV bias loses low bits when added to/subtracted from differently placed atlas rows.
  `SpriteTexture::LocalUV`, instance flag32 and both shader/CPU paths preserve the
  fractional offset. `world-local-bias-ground/probe-sequence-comparison.json` and
  `world-local-bias-plain/previous-reference-comparison.json` now match every pixel.
- New `--verify-world-atlas` recaptures one fixed world around repack, clear and forced
  half-atlas-row placement, comparing every RGBA byte and ID and retaining all four
  PAMs. Optional `OPENTT3D_WORLD_PIXEL=X,Y` records a screen pixel's owner and immutable
  mesh/instance details. Native `world-atlas-local-bias-{vulkan,gl}` and Linux
  `world-atlas-local-bias-validation` pass alongside the industry/ordering checks.
  CPU tests independently demonstrate old offset cancellation and protect local

## Triangle visibility, asynchronous preparation and capture follow-up (September25)

- Pixel visibility now tests conservative triangle edge/sample intervals as well as
  block/AABB bounds, caching shared position projections. It retains uncertain and
  near-plane cases; the paused wide tree stream falls to17,264,058 original vertices
  without changing any of the4,096,000 reference pixels. Procedural rail/fence meshes
  use the same path only after a complete lossless dyadic-lattice roundtrip.
- Background jobs own value-only transforms and immutable mesh references. Camera
  generations cancel stale jobs; returned per-transform geometry can be adopted even
  when live batch order changes. Tests cover cancellation, snapshot ownership and
  synchronous-reference equality. Diagnostic/readback checks remain synchronous;
  `OPENTT3D_VOXEL_CULL_SYNC=1` forces that mode for controlled measurements.
- Profile-guided default tree capture uses fixed source binding/log tables and avoids
  RGBA source-chart work for qualified base-set voxel trees. Relative child offsets
  are fetched lazily when needed. Close/distant, ordering/ground/picking and complete
  paused-world comparisons pass; the full495 regression above covers the later code.

## GL palette stalls and exact opacity partitioning (2026-09-23, 12:41 UTC)

- `gl-resident-wide-profile/cpu-sample.txt` reproduced the wide slowdown:828/1,780
  main-thread samples were inside the world palette's `glTexSubImage2D`, with825
  waiting for the driver to finish the in-use resource. Frame-local256-colour textures
  and byte-exact unchanged-palette detection remove that resource hazard.
- `gl-frame-palette-wide-profile/cpu-sample.txt` then exposed634 main-thread samples
  in the UI's `glTexSubImage1D`. The driver retains complete palette revisions across
  partial updates, uploads a retired frame slot and publishes it with the completed
  UI texture. Consumer fences now include the hardware cursor; CPU waits stay before
  the game-state lock. Original UI shaders and palette arithmetic remain unchanged.
- `gl-frame-palette-validation` and `gl-ui-palette-validation` on native/Linux GL
  pass twelve local changing/returning-colour GPU snapshots across both frame slots,
  full renderer/picking checks and three actual radio-beacon phases. The probes do
  not write the game's palette. New counters: `palette_upload_ms` and
  `ui_palette_upload_ms`. The intermediate wide GL checkpoints remain failures at
  28.432/28.572fps; they expose the next redundant draw cost, not completion.
- GL now partitions clearly opaque and translucent instances as Vulkan does, while
  preserving mesh/within-pass order. The new overlapping-colour shared-mesh probe
  exposed a real0.99-boundary problem: Linux GL `gl-opacity-partition-validation`
  failed, its `OPENTT3D_GL_SPLIT_OPACITY=0` control passed, and the existing Vulkan
  path failed with3,565 differing colour pixels/zero ID differences. Constant alpha
  interpolation can cross the unchanged shader cutoff.
- The shared batcher now counts/scatters both passes by mesh, retaining a complete
  unpartitioned mesh group near the cutoff so the shader still decides coverage in
  original order. The narrow classification guard changes neither alpha values nor
  pixel-comparison tolerance. CPU regression covers the boundary, adjacent floats,
  interleaved order and resetting the group on a later scene. Native GL and Linux
  GL/Vulkan `*-opacity-boundary-corrected` pass exact colour/IDs;5,544 tree views pass.
- `gl-palette-opacity-final-40bpp/comparison.json` and `-32bpp/comparison.json`
  compare the complete2560×1600 screens with pre-optimization CPU references:
  **8,192,000 RGB pixels, zero differences** after all palette/partition changes.
- Full suites **163/163 macOS /164/164 Linux**, renderer **1,070,652 assertions /
  67cases** pass. Catalogue remains79 volumes; vehicle palette matrix1,088 poses.
  The wide GL workload improves to50.834fps but retains600overruns; the6,000-frame
  aircraft Cab averages60.003fps with16overruns. Native Vulkan wide57.953fps also
  remains below target. See `PERFORMANCE.md`; no full performance/fidelity approval.

## GPU-resident OpenGL viewport/UI composition (2026-09-23, 11:36 UTC)

- Replaced normal full colour/ID readback and CPU copying with per-viewport GL
  colour/ID/depth targets. Dirty-region composition preserves original clipping;
  the upstream UI/40bpp palette shaders run unchanged over the viewport textures.
  Picking reads one requested ID pixel. Explicit reference/viewport/large-image
  captures retain the original readback path.
- Cocoa renders/composes in its backing context and shares only the completed
  texture with the layer context. Producer/consumer GPU fences protect reuse;
  explicit context activation protects viewport rendering/picking from a prior
  layer callback. Two frame-completion fences bound queued work, with normal waits
  before the game-state lock. GL drivers upload before Paint; Windows release is
  idempotent like the SDL/Cocoa implementations. Windows runtime remains untested.
- `screenshot presented` now also captures the GL composition texture, before the
  hardware cursor. This proves the GPU-composed image, not foreground/display timing.
  `--readback-presentation` selects the original CPU path for controlled comparisons.
- Native/Linux `gl-resident-first-validation` pass target resize/retirement,
  independent viewport IDs, actual tile picking and154,300 exact CPU-reference RGBA
  pixels covering overlapping/clipped regions, UI masks and odd/resized dimensions.
  Added partial-alpha coverage reproduced a double-premultiplication defect in
  `gl-resident-alpha-regression`; GL_ONE source blending corrects it. Native/Linux
  `gl-resident-alpha-corrected` and Linux `gl-resident-bounded-validation` pass the
  expanded strict check. No comparison tolerance was added.
- Full paused2560×1600 captures match CPU composition exactly in both blitters:
  `gl-resident-alpha-corrected/comparison.json` (40bpp) and
  `gl-resident-rgb-presentation/comparison.json` (32bpp), **8,192,000 RGB pixels**.
  Native/Linux32bpp renderer/picking checks pass. `gl-resident-tiled-screenshot`
  produces5000×1600 through the streamed readback path. Linux
  `gl-resident-vulkan-regression` passes core/synchronization, renderer and picking.
- Matched airport600-frame control/resident runs: work p9517.274→6.241ms,
  74→0overruns; readback11.161→0ms p95 and CPU composition3.152→0ms. Movingfullscreen
  aircraft6,000-frame CPU control averages40.151fps,p9530.635ms,6,000overruns.

  superseded for sustained claims. Details and caveats are in `PERFORMANCE.md`.
- Latest full suites162/162 macOS and163/163 Linux; renderer1,070,604/66cases,

### MoltenVK integer-attachment warning investigated

The pinned v1.4.2 source emits its misleading “Blending is enabled” warning whenever
an attachment format lacks blending support, without checking `pCA->blendEnable` in
that warning branch. The renderer explicitly disables blending for R32_UINT and its
colour/ID/transparent-picking checks pass. This explains the two startup messages;
it does not require enabling unsupported blending or suppressing driver diagnostics.
Source: [MVKPipeline.mm v1.4.2, lines 1980–1995](https://github.com/KhronosGroup/MoltenVK/blob/v1.4.2/MoltenVK/MoltenVK/GPUObjects/MVKPipeline.mm#L1980-L1995).

## Depot and crossing checkpoint

- Inspected **108** resolved depot/crossing source components, all six depot family
  turntables and all four railway crossing families in open/barred states. Depots
  have actual open maintenance bays, inner walls, thick roofs/gables, windows,
  roof mechanisms, company trim and shared track profiles clipped at the rear wall.
  Later passes added buttresses/rear details and roof framing, removed painted
  structural beams from road/tram roof crops, and added tram inlays/contact wires.
- Crossing artwork is replaced by clean terrain/asphalt, modeled posts/lenses,
  road markings and shared volume railway components. Recessed running surfaces
  keep road lanes clear; maglev guide walls leave gaps that close only when the
  original barred flag is set. Foundation, ground state, catenary, bridge and
  reservation decisions remain selected by upstream drawing callbacks.
- **96** depot views pass company recolouring, transparent-wall picking and exact
  CPU/instance component materials. **128** crossing views cover rail systems,
  axes, open/barred states and tram inlays, with warning-lamp and material checks.
  Native full suite **134/134**, Linux **135/135**; renderer subset **20,842 assertions
  in 38 cases**. Final asphalt material scale was also rechecked on both backends.
- Passing artifacts: `depot-first-geometry`, `depot-family-{1…5}`,
  `depot-crossing-first`, `depot-crossing-refined`,
  `infrastructure-family-{0…3}-{0,1}`, `crossing-asphalt-refinement`,
  `build-linux-user/depot-crossing-validation`, and `crossing-asphalt-validation`.
  The Linux runs enable 40bpp and Vulkan core/synchronization validation; the broad
  run also verifies rails/stations/tunnels/live bore, actual tile picking and cameras.
- `rail-road-traffic-fixture` adds a road depot and a bus which must traverse its
  crossing. `rail-road-active-crossing-fixture` moves that route to **(85,16)**,
  between the rail stations so the train operates the crossing repeatedly. The
  successful manifest reports train **0**, bus **2**, `journey_verified: true` and
  `bus_crossing_verified: true`. All construction and traffic use public NoAI APIs.
- `crossing-transition-trace` uses bounded, renderer-only tile observations to
  require an actual **same-tile state change**, not two unrelated static states.
  The moving fullscreen bus view passes at approximately 60 fps, with one brief
  frame-budget overrun; the actual presentation was inspected. See `PERFORMANCE.md`.
- Reviewed `rail_cmd.cpp`, `road_cmd.cpp` and the read-only road-layout accessor in
  `road_cmd.h`; no command handlers were changed. Boundary count **106** source files.
  Remaining work includes model refinement/all climates, road-stop geometry,
  bridge rail joins, further construction/Cab passes and the broader release gates.

## Live railway signals and catenary checkpoint

  upstream callbacks. Bridge and half-depot wires retain their resolved context;

  only the public NoAI API. The resulting layout at **(52,8)** contains four-platform
  and perpendicular stations, two depots, crossings, an electric bridge, all six
  signal types and tunnel **(75,16)→(83,16)** at floor 16. Its locomotive must reach
  the eastern terminus over the bridge/tunnel and return before the fixture passes.
  Both macOS and Linux report `journey_verified: true`.
- Final checks: **132/132 macOS**, **133/133 Linux**, **20,442 renderer assertions /
  36 cases**, existing Python suites **8/8** and **7/7**, and 98 reviewed source

- `build-macos/electric-rail-stock-roundtrip` re-saves the moving fixture with
  official **15.3**. `electric-rail-cab-1800` reloads that result in OpenTT3D and
  captures a moving fullscreen train inside the tunnel. This proves that round trip,

## Geometry-aware map interaction checkpoint

- Captured terrain, structures, trees, transport and raised ground details now
  carry their owning tile, so a roof/wall click selects that structure's tile rather
  than the terrain intersected behind it. The upstream `TranslateXYToTileCoord`
  entry point routes through geometry ownership; camera anchoring retains its
  separate terrain-ray behavior. Vehicle body clicks keep their own ID namespace.
- `InstanceData` remains **96 bytes**. The numeric ID payload preserves all 24 bits
  of a legal map index; a separate flag selects the tile namespace (`0x80000000`).
  Source mirroring remains a separate flag. CPU/GPU checks include tile zero,
  indices beyond 2²³ and the final 2²⁴−1 index, plus switching back to vehicle IDs.
- A loaded-world probe checks **64 roof/wall pixels** where the old terrain-only
  ray selects the wrong tile. It exercises the actual viewport input entry point
  across four rotations, then the GPU-resident one-pixel path. Geometry cannot be
  mistaken for a vehicle. Tagged and untagged captures have **identical colours**.
- Last-displayed-frame ownership is used for input, with a per-pixel/frame cache.
  Active-hover picking performance, construction drags, all secondary viewports
  and broader native interaction remain later playtest work.
- Full suites pass **130/130 macOS**, **131/131 Linux**; renderer subset **20,050
  assertions in 34 cases**. Evidence: `build-macos/geometry-tile-picking-first`,
  `tile-picking-opengl`, and `build-linux-user/tile-picking-validation`. Linux uses
  40bpp and Vulkan core/synchronization validation, including bridge/fence/
  foundation/tunnel and camera regression checks. The minimum-time goal is active.

```sh
python3 tools/opentt3d/smoke.py --build-dir build-macos \
  --output build-macos/check-map-picking --backend vulkan \
  --savegame media/baseset/opntitle.dat --verify-renderer --verify-tile-picking
```

## Extended development pass: input, camera, fences and foundations

The complete timed objective and repeated-pass ledger are in [ACTIVE_GOAL.md](ACTIVE_GOAL.md).
The minimum time has not yet elapsed; this is an intermediate checkpoint.

- Removed obsolete build objects/executables and shared six HighDef archive paths
  through one checksum-verified project-local cache. Free space initially rose
  from 6.5 GiB to about 14 GiB. The downloader and stock-binary smoke staging now
  hard-link immutable data where supported and atomically copy across devices.
- Cocoa input now routes through the wrapper view, reconciles missed entry events,
  uses button-event positions, and releases relative capture/buttons on focus loss.
  Transparent native cursor data has owned storage and is reused.
  `build-macos/input-recovery-focus-transfer/` passes **32 actual window focus/capture
  cycles and four fullscreen transitions**, using `--macos-bundle --verify-native-input`.
  Later foreground probes report `front=loginwindow`; they are not passing interaction
  evidence. Renderer/offscreen checks continue while the desktop is locked.
- All interactive world cameras use a fixed **40° vertical FOV**. Cab wheel zoom
  is disabled. Zoom moves the eye in 3D about its anchor, and a terrain-relative
  reparameterization preserves the actual pose while recovering dolly range after
  an elevated orbit focus. Legacy zoom/DPI bookkeeping cannot reset the 3D zoom.
  The ordinary close limit is **−6**. Reference-gallery cameras retain their own
  framing; older measurements below describe older camera configurations.
- Farm and rail fences use explicit posts, rails, hedge/stone sections and chain-link
  wires. Their feet follow the same terrain triangles, including sloped edges.
  Source references were inspected for all seven styles. Gate paint leaking onto
  hedge end-caps was corrected by separating materials; blue rail posts and wire
  metal have explicitly selected source patches. **532** GPU views pass.
- Foundations now construct the difference between the original and supported
  surfaces on every exposed side, with internal half-tile retaining walls. Material
  charts repeat over 16×8-unit wall sections rather than stretching a silhouette.
  **264** views cover 66 valid slope/foundation combinations, including upstream
  rail-foundation choices and two-part steep foundations.
- Upper half-tile ground draws only its upper triangle, preserving the lower
  ground/water submitted earlier. Natural terrain charts and geometric material
  lighting are supported on both backends. Directional detail already painted into
  source grass and other ground illustrations still needs dedicated surface-detail work.
- Full suites at the foundation checkpoint: **118/118 macOS**, **119/119 Linux**; renderer subset:
  **9,646 assertions in 22 cases**. **8** asset/compiler and **7** downloader/PNG
  tests pass. Linux Vulkan synchronization validation passes with 40bpp and
  camera, foundation, fence and bridge checks.

Artifacts include `build-linux-user/foundation-fence-validation/`,
`build-macos/foundation-volume-first/`, `foundation-half-review/`,
`foundation-steep-review/`, `fence-gate-components/`, `fence-chain-components/`
and `fences-flat-terrain-charts/`. Geometry visibility and these initial visual

## Initial volumetric bridges

- Upstream bridge drawing supplies type, piece, axis, ramp direction, selected
  sprite/palette and support placement through scoped presentation metadata.
  Existing ground-height, blocked-pillar and visibility decisions are retained.
  The back sections omitted by the fixed isometric view are included during 3D capture.
- At that checkpoint the native and Linux full suites passed **116/116** and **117/117** respectively.
  Renderer checks include all four ramps against upstream vehicle slopes and
  finite, outward-wound bridge faces after axis reflection and slope shear.
- **832** synthetic assembly views cover 13 types, four transport materials,
  both axes, flat/sloped heads and four camera directions. Additional checks
  verify that half-pillars partition complete columns and transparent supports
  do not intercept picking. Per-component materials/IDs match CPU-expanded
  reference geometry exactly.
- Expanded reference vertices can retain sprite-local UVs. This fixes the
  one-pixel atlas-add/subtract precision loss exposed by the Linux 40bpp test;
  the nearest-sampling rule and comparison tolerance were not relaxed.
  Composite colours at coincident internal faces are order-dependent, so exact
  material comparisons isolate components; eliminating those joins remains art work.
- Passing artifacts: `build-linux-user/bridge-local-uv-validation/` (Vulkan
  core/synchronization validation, 40bpp, industry and loaded-world camera checks),
  `build-macos/bridge-local-uv-temperate/` (Vulkan assembly gallery/checks),
  `build-macos/bridge-local-uv-opengl/` (rotated, tilted live bridge and camera checks).
- `bridge-geometry-first` and `bridge-geometry-temperate` are failed half-pillar
  experiments. The Linux `bridge-components-40-diagnostic` exposed reference UV
  precision loss; it is not a passing artifact.
- Eight asset/compiler tests, three screenshot-harness tests and the 71-file
  presentation-boundary check pass. CI now includes bridge checks and live focus.
- The subsequent CPU atlas-storage reuse pass passes three consecutive exact
  colour/ID relocation checks, bridge checks and loaded-world navigation in
  `build-linux-user/bridge-pooled-atlas-validation/` (40bpp, Vulkan core/sync
  validation) and `build-macos/bridge-pooled-atlas-opengl/`.

The models remain work in progress: component UVs/proportions, tubular detail,
coincident joins, catenary, aqueduct fixtures and custom layouts need further review.
Other infrastructure remains incomplete. Post-bridge fullscreen results and the
remaining moving-Cab stalls are recorded in [PERFORMANCE.md](PERFORMANCE.md).

No industry review flags are approved. Remaining mature tiles, construction,

## Clicked-point tilt and unlimited first-person distance

- Middle-button horizontal/vertical drag now rotates/tilts rigidly about the
  terrain point picked on press. The point's screen position and physical eye
  radius stay fixed. Pitch is clamped to 3°–89° and movement that would place the
  eye underground is constrained. Releasing preserves the complete orientation.
- Cab middle-drag performs yaw/pitch free-look around the moving eye; wheel zoom
  adjusts the lens without cancelling follow. Reset restores the default tilt/look.
- Both backends now use **infinite-far reversed depth**. Vulkan writes clip Z
  directly; OpenGL uses a 32-bit floating depth buffer and explicit reversed
  fragment depth to preserve distant precision. There is no fixed far plane.
- Frustum/map-box intersection replaces finite corner-ray bounds, so horizon
  views collect distant terrain across the actual map. Picking has no maximum
  distance, uses double-precision entry/traversal, and handles integer-height
  cell walls at shallow angles. The former 128-unit Cab label cutoff is removed.
- Infinite-water exterior geometry has homogeneous vertices at infinity rather
  than a distant finite rectangle. `build-macos/orbit-unlimited-water/` was
  visually inspected: the ocean reaches a continuous horizon when tilted low.
- Current suites: **114/114 native**, **115/115 Linux**, **4,985 renderer assertions
  in 18 cases**, **8/8 asset/compiler** and **3/3 screenshot** Python tests.
- GPU checks cover colour, ordered depth and picking at **8,192**, **131,072** and
  **1,000,000** world units; horizon water in all four directions; actual loaded-map
  capture/picking/labels beyond 4,096 units; clicked-point tilt, release, screenshots,
  tilted pan/zoom, and Cab look/zoom.
- Final artifacts: `build-linux-user/unlimited-final-validation/` (Khronos core
  and synchronization validation, 40bpp, Cab and both-axis input),
  `build-macos/unlimited-final-opengl/` and `build-macos/industry-cooling-tower/`.
  The early Linux `orbit-unlimited-validation` and `orbit-unlimited-precise-picking`
  runs failed the shallow terrain-picking check; the final run passes it.

```sh
python3 tools/opentt3d/smoke.py --build-dir build-macos \
  --output build-macos/check-tilt --backend vulkan \
  --savegame media/baseset/opntitle.dat --orbit-drag 110 -500 --verify-renderer
```

The unlimited view increases the Cab workload. Actual-camera frame caching removes
duplicate following-viewport renders, a bounded 96 MiB decoded-source cache avoids
re-decoding evicted materials, and height-sorted bulk repacking reduces compaction
cost. All integer zooms and low-angle orbit meet the measured fixed-view budget;
moving-Cab texture/loading tails remain in [PERFORMANCE.md](PERFORMANCE.md).

The initial industry checkpoint contained mature geometry for **10/175 tile
definitions** (the subsequent expansion is above). Four-view cooling-tower and
mine-head galleries were inspected in `build-macos/industry-cooling-tower/` and
`industry-mine-head/`. Side/rear UV distortion and structural detail still need
correction; these profiles are not approved completed release assets.

- `vehicles.json` explicitly binds all **256** vanilla definitions. The compiler
  rejects missing/duplicate bindings and cyclic assembly inheritance. Eight
  asset/compiler Python tests and three screenshot tests pass.
- Current full CTest runs remain **111/111 macOS**, **112/112 Linux ARM64**;
  renderer unit checks have **1,813 assertions in 15 cases**.
- GPU model checks exercise both cargo states and all eight directions for each
  climate's available definitions: **2,064 temperate**, **1,968 arctic**,
  **2,064 tropical**, **1,424 Toyland** poses, totaling **7,520**. Artifacts are
  `build-macos/vehicle-verify-{temperate,arctic,tropic,toyland}/`.
- The loaded Toyland scene reports **379 visible authored vehicle bodies** in
  `vehicle-resolved-bindings`; remaining reference planes are still logged.
- `build-linux-user/vehicle-local-uv-validation/` passes the vehicle suite, GPU
  material tests and Vulkan synchronization validation. The first
  `vehicle-vulkan-validation` attempt exposed atlas relocation rounding and is
  a failed artifact. Local UV interpolation plus integer texel addressing fixed it.
- OpenGL passes the same vehicle/scene checks in
  `build-macos/vehicle-opengl-verification/` and
  `build-linux-user/vehicle-opengl-local-uv/` (the latter includes the sampling fix).
- Viewport/atlas tests additionally compare explicit vehicle UVs with CPU-expanded
  geometry, and check black shadow composition with transparent picking.
- `build-macos/vehicle-era-water-edge/` was visually inspected after the sampling
  change: the generated infinite-water setting persists through save/reload, and
  textured ocean covers the exterior at the map corner.
- `industry-source-catalogue/renderer3d-reference/` contains **700** industry
  tile/stage records and review sheets. Those are source references, not 3D assets.

Vehicle reference export now filters by active climate. The earlier full-table
exports included unavailable vehicles whose sprite slots are reused in another
climate, making those comparisons misleading. Per-climate exports and galleries
must be used for visual approval. Basic volume coverage and passing pick tests do
not establish final silhouettes, materials, liveries or animation fidelity.

```sh
python3 tools/opentt3d/smoke.py --build-dir build-macos \
  --output build-macos/check-vehicles --backend vulkan --climate arctic \
  --verify-vehicles --export-vehicles --gallery-vehicle 1
python3 tools/opentt3d/smoke.py --build-dir build-macos \
  --output build-macos/check-loaded-wagon --backend vulkan \
  --savegame build-macos/vehicles-temperate-reference/save/smoke-state.sav \
  --gallery-vehicle 29 --loaded-vehicle
```

Fullscreen performance was rerun at the current **3024×1964** backing size with
the vehicle geometry. All integer zooms average about 60 fps with no work overruns
in their 300-frame runs. A longer moving Cab run still has rare 22.4 ms outliers.
GUI/DPI rescaling no longer silently replaces continuous camera zoom with a legacy
integer level. Details and valid artifact paths are in [PERFORMANCE.md](PERFORMANCE.md).

## Vulkan, instancing and fullscreen performance — 2026-09-20

- Native macOS: **111/111 CTest cases**. Linux ARM64: **112/112**.
  Renderer subset: **1,810 assertions in 15 cases**. Four asset-compiler tests,
  three PNG harness tests and the 66-file presentation boundary check pass.
- Cocoa/MoltenVK and SDL/Mesa Vulkan pass depth/ID, transparent click-through,
  uneven screenshot-tile seams, instanced versus expanded geometry, textured atlas
  relocation, viewport resize, one-pixel picking and target retirement checks.
- `build-linux-user/vulkan-atlas-final/` explicitly enables Khronos core and
  synchronization validation, runs with 40bpp animation and a secondary Cab
  viewport, and reports no validation errors.
- `build-linux-novulkan/smoke-fallback/` is a fresh **OPTION_VULKAN=OFF** build
  running the OpenGL checks. `build-macos/opengl-final-check/` verifies native GL.
- Vulkan harness captures now read the **actual presented swapchain**. Earlier
  `vulkan-first-verified` screenshots used the CPU screenshot path and did not
  establish live composition correctness. `vulkan-presented-ready`,
  `vulkan-final-40-ui` and the benchmark captures do.
- Official 15.3 reference `build-macos/vulkan-stock-ui-reference/` matches
  `vulkan-terrain-verified` (32bpp) and `vulkan-final-40-ui` (40bpp) exactly in
  **254,448 toolbar/status-bar pixels**. Terrain instancing also preserved all
  **4,096,000 pixels** of the paused pre-instancing Vulkan capture.
- All nine integer zoom levels −3…5 were measured for 300 frames each at
  **2704×1756 fullscreen**, with a running dense title fixture. Each averaged
  approximately 60 fps with no work-budget overruns. The 1800-frame moving Cab
  test still has rare outliers; see [PERFORMANCE.md](PERFORMANCE.md).

The negative-zoom matrix initially exposed unsigned console parsing: requests
for −3/−2/−1 were ignored. Those `vulkan-zoom-matrix--*` artifacts are invalid
close-zoom evidence. Signed parsing is fixed; `vulkan-zoom-signed--*` contains the
correct reruns, and the harness now asserts measured zoom equals requested zoom.
Direct presentation capture also caught an empty startup frame; it now draws the
dirty windows first and rejects captures without GPU world regions.

```sh
python3 tools/opentt3d/smoke.py --build-dir build-macos \
  --output build-macos/check-vulkan --backend vulkan \
  --savegame media/baseset/opntitle.dat --verify-renderer --blitter 40bpp-anim
docker run --init --rm --user "$(id -u):$(id -g)" -e HOME=/tmp \
  -v "$PWD:/workspace" -w /workspace opentt3d-dev \
  xvfb-run -a python3 tools/opentt3d/smoke.py --build-dir build-linux-user \
  --output build-linux-user/check-vulkan --backend vulkan --vulkan-validation \
  --savegame media/baseset/opntitle.dat --verify-renderer --first-person auto \
  --blitter 40bpp-anim --benchmark-frames 30 --timeout 180
python3 tools/opentt3d/compare_ui.py \
  build-macos/vulkan-stock-ui-reference/screenshot/smoke.png \
  build-macos/vulkan-final-40-ui/screenshot/smoke.png \
  --rect 164,0,2232,70 --rect 164,1556,2232,44
```

These are local correctness/performance results, not a completed artwork or
cross-platform release gate. The updated CI definition includes Vulkan validation
and a build-local MoltenVK fetch; it has not yet been run as a full hosted matrix.

## Earlier camera controls, ocean and branding update

At this checkpoint, native tests: **107/107**; Linux ARM64/Mesa: **108/108**. The renderer
subset now passes **1,081 assertions in 11 cases**, including square-pixel lens
calibration, bounded building lean, arbitrary yaw, first-person projection and
frame-rate-independent interpolation.

Both native Apple OpenGL and SDL/Mesa pass the loaded-world checks for smooth
zoom anchoring, persistent middle-button yaw, extended close zoom, first-person
entry/exit/heading interpolation and camera snapshots. The smoke harness invokes
the real vehicle-window Cab button and records its activation.

Inspected artifacts include `build-macos/vehicle-cab-first/`,
`build-linux-user/camera-cab-mesa/`, `build-macos/infinite-ocean-instant/` and
`build-macos/branded-menu/`. The cold generated-world capture can still contain
the final generation dialog: reload its saved fixture to inspect the finished
world. Smoke centring now explicitly uses `scrollto instant`; earlier plain
`scrollto` commands scheduled a smooth pan that the following zoom could cancel.

The clean branded menu is in `build-macos/branded-menu-clean/`. Confirmed Cocoa
borderless fullscreen measurement/capture is in
`build-macos/perf-borderless-fullscreen/`; the measured **9.28 fps** is a failing
performance baseline, not a completed 60 fps result.

The executable is now `opentt3d` (`opentt3d.exe` on Windows). The build target and
test binary remain `openttd` and `openttd_test` for upstream build compatibility.
Branding assets are reproducibly compiled from `assets/branding/opentt3d.svg`.
The fork uses the existing disabled-survey path instead of prompting for or
transmitting data to OpenTTD's survey service.

Vulkan device enumeration through pinned MoltenVK succeeds on Apple M3 Pro.
At that checkpoint the game backend was OpenGL; subsequent Vulkan work is above. See
[PERFORMANCE.md](PERFORMANCE.md).

## Perspective / OpenGFX2 HighDef update — 2026-09-20

The current artwork pin is **OpenGFX2 High Def 0.8.1**. The sections below this
update retain the earlier orthographic/OpenGFX 8.0 evidence as historical results.

Current checks:

- macOS ARM64: **104/104 CTest cases**; eight renderer cases cover projection,
  anchoring, cropping, instancing and isolated source-pixel registration.
- Linux ARM64 container: **105/105 CTest cases**, including the ICU test.
- Four legacy asset-compiler tests, three PNG-completion tests and the
  presentation-file boundary check pass.
- Native Apple OpenGL 4.1 and Mesa/llvmpipe OpenGL 4.5 both pass GPU depth/ID,
  transparent colour blending and click-through verification.
- Uneven 73×59-pixel GPU crops match the full synthetic framebuffer exactly,
  including colour and object IDs at seams.
- Loaded-world tests exercise cursor-anchored zoom/drag across all four rotations
  and three starting zoom levels. The native title fixture passes 184 anchor
  checks and 64 live vehicle-follow/screenshot-camera checks; its Linux run passes
  160 anchor checks and 64 follow checks at the smaller backing resolution.
- A complete **18,000×128** PNG was produced by the tiled screenshot path and
  decoded with Pillow. The smoke harness now waits for PNG `IEND` and validates
  requested dimensions. The earlier `highdef-large-18000` artifact is truncated
  and is **not** evidence of a successful large screenshot.
- Official OpenTTD 15.3 and the HighDef/perspective build match in all 254,448
  toolbar/status-bar pixels, with both the 32bpp and 40bpp development captures.
- A current paused fixture was saved by this build, loaded/re-saved by official
  15.3, then loaded/rendered/verified again by this build with rotation 2.

Reproduce a GPU and navigation check (always choose a fresh output directory):

```sh
python3 tools/opentt3d/smoke.py --build-dir build-macos \
  --output build-macos/check-perspective --savegame media/baseset/opntitle.dat \
  --rotation 1 --verify-renderer
python3 tools/opentt3d/smoke.py --build-dir build-macos \
  --output build-macos/check-large --savegame build-macos/check-perspective/save/smoke-state.sav \
  --screenshot-size 18000 128 --timeout 180
```

The harness copies `.dat` fixtures to an isolated `.sav` filename because upstream
`-g` selects its loader by extension. Without this, the title fixture opens the
menu rather than executing the loaded-game script.

Representative artifacts:

- `build-macos/highdef-birch-corrected/`: corrected birch gallery and verified native frame.
- `build-macos/highdef-rounded-models/` and `highdef-alpha-states/`: curved-model and alpha-state review artifacts.
- `build-macos/highdef-title-navigation-fixed/`: dense title-fixture navigation/follow checks.
- `build-macos/highdef-large-complete/`: finished large PNG and bounded-size preview.
- `build-macos/highdef-stock-ui/`: official HighDef UI reference.
- `build-macos/highdef-roundtrip-stock/` and `highdef-roundtrip-back/`: current save round trip.
- `build-linux-user/highdef-materials-final/`: current Mesa title-fixture checks.

These checks do not establish completed artwork, all construction/overlay
interactions, simulation replay equivalence, multiplayer interoperability or
release-package portability. Those gates remain open in [STATUS.md](STATUS.md).

## Earlier prototype evidence

## Environments

- Native macOS 26.6.2, Apple M3 Pro, Apple OpenGL 4.1.
- Docker Desktop Linux ARM64, Debian bookworm, Mesa llvmpipe OpenGL 4.5.
- OpenTTD baseline: 15.3 (`14ec60f248547d4d062a1160f0fc26d742319888`).
- OpenGFX: 8.0, downloaded with the published SHA-256 verified.

All generated configs, saves, downloads and screenshots are under ignored build
directories. Interactive smoke tests use `-X -c <isolated-config> -x` to avoid
the user's installed content and configuration.

## Automated tests

```sh
python3 tools/opentt3d/build.py --build-dir build-macos
python3 -m unittest discover -s tools/assets -p 'test_*.py'
python3 tools/opentt3d/check_boundary.py
```

Results: 99 native CTest cases, 100 container CTest cases (the Linux build adds
an ICU test), and four asset compiler tests passed. These include the original
AI regression fixtures. Renderer tests check upstream projection agreement,
projection/unprojection at every rotation and zoom, immutable model instancing,
outward normals, non-degenerate triangles and glTF geometry preservation.

The file-boundary guard is a review aid, not a proof of simulation equivalence.

## Native rendering

```sh
python3 tools/opentt3d/smoke.py --build-dir build-macos \
  --output build-macos/smoke-example --rotation 1
```

The harness uses existing OpenTTD console scripts and its own screenshot API.
It fails if the 3D backend did not initialize or reported a rendering error.
Both `32bpp-optimized` and `40bpp-anim` were exercised locally.

The initial new-game script can capture the final world-generation progress
dialog. For a settled UI comparison, load the saved fixture in a second run:

```sh
python3 tools/opentt3d/smoke.py --build-dir build-macos \
  --output build-macos/smoke-loaded \
  --savegame build-macos/smoke-example/save/smoke-state.sav
```

For Linux rendering in Docker, use `--init` so Xvfb's startup signal is handled:

```sh
docker run --init --rm --user "$(id -u):$(id -g)" -e HOME=/tmp \
  -v "$PWD:/workspace" -w /workspace opentt3d-dev \
  xvfb-run -a python3 tools/opentt3d/smoke.py \
  --build-dir build-linux --output build-linux/smoke-example --rotation 1
```

## Official-binary save interoperability

`fetch_reference.py` downloads the official universal Mac 15.3 zip and verifies
SHA-256 `c2ac22ab3ac9ac1e82a8d9029b7e4ab069e425904420076605d860e64460f579`.
The original app signature was also verified with `codesign --verify --deep --strict`.

```sh
python3 tools/opentt3d/fetch_reference.py build-stock
python3 tools/opentt3d/save_probe.py \
  --executable build-stock/OpenTTD.app/Contents/MacOS/openttd \
  --savegame build-macos/smoke-example/save/smoke-state.sav \
  --baseset build-macos/baseset/opengfx-8.0.tar \
  --output build-macos/roundtrip-stock
python3 tools/opentt3d/smoke.py --build-dir build-macos \
  --savegame build-macos/roundtrip-stock/save/roundtrip.sav \
  --output build-macos/roundtrip-back
```

One generated, paused vanilla 128×128 fixture passed this round trip. This does
not establish complete save coverage, gameplay equivalence or multiplayer
compatibility; those remain separate gates.

## UI pixels

Official OpenTTD and OpenTT3D loaded the same paused fixture, with OpenGFX 8.0,
the same resolution, language and UI settings. At the native 2560×1600 backing
resolution, these rectangles were compared:

- Toolbar: `164,0,2232,70` — 156,240 pixels.
- Status bar: `164,1556,2232,44` — 98,208 pixels.

Both the 32bpp and 40bpp OpenTT3D captures matched official OpenTTD exactly in
both regions. Window dialogs, other UI scales and languages require additional
fixtures. Rectangle coordinates must be adjusted to the actual backing resolution.

```sh
python3 tools/opentt3d/compare_ui.py reference.png actual.png \
  --rect 164,0,2232,70 --rect 164,1556,2232,44
```
